<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a deterministic algorithm that computes the complete zero-threshold winning set of a finite mean-payoff game with arbitrary signed integer edge weights encoded in binary. For total explicit input length <i>L</i>, it uses $`2^{O((\log(L+2))^2)}`$ bit operations. A reduction also computes the exact rational value at every vertex and globally optimal positional strategies for both players within the same quasipolynomial bound.

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): A uniform deterministic algorithm returns exactly the set $$U=\bigl\{v\in V:\exists\text{ Max strategy }\sigma\ \forall\text{ Min strategies }\tau, \ \mathop{\mathrm{MP}}_w(\pi_{v,\sigma,\tau})\ge0\bigr\}$$ for every game specified above. For an absolute constant $C>0$, the complete computation uses at most $$2^{C(\log_2(L+2))^2}$$ bit operations in a standard model polynomially equivalent to a uniform deterministic Turing machine. The bound includes preprocessing, exact arithmetic, recursion, and output of the entire set $U$.
  - Explanation: Computes the entire zero-threshold winning set with a deterministic quasipolynomial bound in binary input length, including preprocessing and output.
- **C02** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-accepted-box)): For the operator in Equation <a href="#eq:operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:operator">[eq:operator]</a> and arbitrary finite integer bounds $A\le u$, every subsolution $x$ and supersolution $y$ in $[A,u]$ satisfy $x_i\le y_i$ for all $i\in V$. There is a unique $h\in[A,u]$ that is both a subsolution and a supersolution. It is precisely the unique fixed point of $$T_{A,u}(z)=\min\{u,\max\{A,F(z)\}\}.$$ Consequently every such pair satisfies $x\le h\le y$.* *The following synchronous iteration finds $h$. Start with $p=A$; evaluate $q=T_{A,u}(p)$; return $p$ if $q=p$, and otherwise set $p=q$ and repeat. It uses at most $$1+\sum_{i\in V}(u_i-A_i)$$ evaluations of $T_{A,u}$, including the final stability test.
  - Explanation: Compares all subsolutions and supersolutions, identifies a unique clipped fixed point, and bounds synchronous iteration including the final stability test.
- **C03** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-game-reduction)): Let $h$ be the box solution in $[0,D_0]^V$. Then $$V_{\mathrm{lo}}=\{i:h_i\le nW\},\qquad V_{\mathrm{hi}}=\{i:h_i\ge D_0-nW\}$$ partition $V$. There is a positional Max strategy under which every play starting in $V_{\mathrm{hi}}$ has original mean payoff at least zero. There is a positional Min strategy under which every play starting in $V_{\mathrm{lo}}$ has original upper limiting average at most $-1/n$. Both assertions allow arbitrary history-dependent opposition.
  - Explanation: Separates the box solution into disjoint boundary bands and supplies positional winning strategies against history-dependent opposition.
- **C04** (construction, [source](#stmt-def-witnesses)): A *Max witness* for $(A,D,a,b)$ is a subsolution $x\in[A,u]$ with support $S_x=\{i:x_i>A_i\}$ and $a(S_x)\le1$. It *claims* each $i$ with $x_i\ge u_i-P$. A *Min witness* is a supersolution $y\in[A,u]$ with support $S_y=\{i:y_i<u_i\}$ and $b(S_y)\le1$. It claims each $i$ with $y_i\le A_i+P$. A witness is *relevant* when it claims at least one index.
  - Explanation: Defines mass-bounded potential witnesses, their boundary claims, and relevance.
- **C05** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-labelling)): For every input in Equation <a href="#eq:recursive-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:recursive-input">[eq:recursive-input]</a>, the procedure $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Label}}(A,D,a,b)$ of Section <a href="#sec:algorithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algorithm">4</a> terminates and returns a vector in $\{+,-\}^V$. It assigns $+$ to every index claimed by any Max witness and $-$ to every index claimed by any Min witness.
  - Explanation: Returns a simultaneous labelling satisfying every qualifying witness, without requiring witnesses as input.
- **C06** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-translation)): Let $(A,D,a,b)$ be an input with $D\ge4096$, let $c-M\le k\le c+M$ be integral, and let $r\in\{q,D/2\}$. For any relevant Max witness $x$, the vector $$x_i'=\max\{k_i-r/2,\ x_i-s_x(k)+r/2\} \label{eq:max-translation}$$ is an integer subsolution in $[k-r/2,k+r/2]$. Its support is $$S_{x'}=\{i:g_i^x(k)<r\}\subseteq S_x.$$ For any positive new Max masses $a'$ with $a'(S_{x'})\le1$, it is a Max witness and claims every $i$ with $g_i^x(k)\le r/32$.* *For any relevant Min witness $y$, the vector $$y_i'=\min\{k_i+r/2,\ y_i+s_y(k)-r/2\} \label{eq:min-translation}$$ is an integer supersolution in the same box, with support $$S_{y'}=\{i:g_i^y(k)<r\}\subseteq S_y.$$ For any positive new Min masses $b'$ with $b'(S_{y'})\le1$, it is a Min witness and claims every $i$ with $g_i^y(k)\le r/32$. In particular, the corresponding unchanged masses $a$ and $b$ always make these translated vectors witnesses.
  - Explanation: Translation and clipping preserve the required potential inequalities; translated supports are contained in parent supports and near-peak coordinates become claims.
- **C08** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-well-formed)): Every recursive child is a valid input of the form Equation <a href="#eq:recursive-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:recursive-input">[eq:recursive-input]</a>, and its box is contained in its parent’s box. In a nonbase call the pivot satisfies $$\begin{aligned} c-M\le k\le c&\qquad\text{during the downward pass},\\ c-M\le k\le c+M&\qquad\text{during the upward pass}. \end{aligned}$$ If child calls terminate, each pass makes at most $1024n+1$ calls.
  - Explanation: Establishes valid nested child boxes and bounded pivot passes, conditional on termination of child calls.
- **C09** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-termination)): The procedure terminates on every input in Equation <a href="#eq:recursive-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:recursive-input">[eq:recursive-input]</a>. Writing $D=2^d$, every child of a nonbase call has smaller rank $$R=d+B(\mu(a,b)).$$ The provisional child in Step 5 is the only child that preserves $B$. Every other child decreases $B$ by at least one.
  - Explanation: Proves termination by a decreasing rank combining width and minimum mass-product budget.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-down)): For every relevant Max witness $x$, its peak $s_x(k)$ is constant throughout Step 3 and at most $D/2$. In particular, no Max witness claims an index in $I_-$. For every Min witness $y$ that has a claim outside $I_-$, $$b\bigl(\{i:g_i^y(k^{\downarrow})<q\}\bigr)>3/4. \label{eq:down-heavy}$$
  - Explanation: Protects Max peaks and claims during downward motion and certifies heavy Min near-peak supports at the stationary call.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-up)): For every relevant Min witness $y$, its peak $s_y(k)$ is constant throughout Step 4 and at most $D/2$. Thus no Min witness claims an index in $I_+$. At the final pivot, $$\begin{aligned} b\bigl(\{i:g_i^y(k^f)<q\}\bigr)&>3/4 &&\text{if }y\text{ has a claim outside }I_-; \label{eq:final-min-heavy}\\ a\bigl(\{i:g_i^x(k^f)<q\}\bigr)&>3/4 &&\text{if }x\text{ has a claim outside }I_+. \label{eq:final-max-heavy} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Protects Min peaks during upward motion, preserves their earlier mass certificate, and certifies heavy Max near-peak supports.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-reweight)): Every parent Max witness with a claim outside $I_+$ remains a Max witness for $(A,D,\bar a,\bar b)$. Every parent Min witness with a claim outside $I_-$ remains a Min witness for that input.
  - Explanation: Preserves every unsettled parent witness under the new masses while retaining its box and operator.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-node-count)): The complete main computation makes at most $$\sum_{\ell=0}^{H}\sum_{j=0}^{\min(\ell,B_0)} \binom{\ell}{j}s^j \ \le\ (H+1)(B_0+1)(1+Hs)^{B_0} \label{eq:node-bound}$$ calls, including the initial call.
  - Explanation: Counts recursive calls by depth and the positions of budget-decreasing edges.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-full-solution)): A uniform deterministic algorithm returns the exact value $\operatorname{val}_w(v)$ as a reduced rational for every $v\in V$ and one positional strategy $\sigma^\star$ for Max and one positional strategy $\tau^\star$ for Min such that, simultaneously for every $v\in V$ and every pair of history-dependent strategies $\sigma,\tau$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{MP}}_w(\pi_{v,\sigma^\star,\tau})\ge\operatorname{val}_w(v), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{MP}}_w(\pi_{v,\sigma,\tau^\star})\le\operatorname{val}_w(v).$$ The complete computation takes $2^{O((\log_2(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations.
  - Explanation: Reconstructs exact rational values and separately recovers globally optimal positional strategies for both players.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-randomized-full-solution)): For the game model of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, a uniform randomized algorithm returns all exact values and both globally optimal positional strategies specified in Corollary <a href="#cor:full-solution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:full-solution">14</a> with probability at least $7/8$ for the entire output. Its running time is $2^{O((\log_2(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations on every random tape.
  - Explanation: Amplifies the companion winning-set interface to guarantee the entire full-solution output with the stated success probability and a bound on every random tape.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-energy-credits)): Uniform deterministic algorithms have the following guarantees.* 1. *Given $v$ and a nonnegative binary integer $c$, decide whether $(v,c)$ is energy-winning. On a positive answer, return a positional Max policy satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:energy-objective" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-objective">[eq:energy-objective]</a> from that start and credit against every history-dependent Min strategy.* 2. *Given $v$, return the exact $c_{\min}(v)$, including the possible answer $\infty$. On a finite answer, return a positional Max policy winning from $v$ at credit $c_{\min}(v)$.* *The fixed-credit computation takes $2^{O((\log_2(L_c+2))^2)}$ bit operations, where $L_c$ is the full explicit input length including the start and credit. The minimum-credit computation takes $2^{O((\log_2(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations for the original game length $L$. Handling every vertex by separate per-start searches, including a separate policy for every finite answer, has the same form of total bit bound.* *Each policy may depend on the queried start and credit; it also wins from that same start at every larger credit.
  - Explanation: Decides prescribed energy credits and computes exact minimum credits, returning a positional policy for each finite answer.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-tropical-computation)): For explicitly listed $m\times n$ matrices $A,B$ and $m$-dimensional vectors $c,d$ over $\mathbb Q\cup\{-\infty\}$, feasibility of $$\max\bigl\{c_i,\max_j(A_{ij}+x_j)\bigr\} \leq \max\bigl\{d_i,\max_j(B_{ij}+x_j)\bigr\} \qquad\text{for every row }i$$ is decidable deterministically in $2^{O((\log(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations, where $L$ is the complete explicit binary input length. Variables range over $\mathbb R\cup\{-\infty\}$; feasibility with all coordinates finite is decidable within the same bound. Equalities between the two displayed maxima are allowed, interpreted as pairs of inequalities. For a homogeneous system ($c=d=-\infty$), one can also compute the maximal finite-coordinate support of a solution, including the empty support. In particular, the same bound decides feasibility of max-atom systems $z\leq\max(x,y)+a$, with rational constants $a$ and finite real, equivalently rational, variables.* *For each fixed integer $k\geq0$, a deterministic algorithm with bit bound $2^{O_k((\log(L+2))^2)}$ decides whether an explicitly listed $m\times n$ matrix over $\mathbb Q\cup\{-\infty\}$, with $m\geq n\geq1$, has tropical rank at least $n-k$. Here tropical rank is the largest order of a square submatrix whose permutation sums have a finite, unique maximum. The rank is zero if no such nonempty submatrix exists.
  - Explanation: Decides tropical feasibility, computes homogeneous maximal finite support, and tests tropical rank near the number of columns when corank is fixed.

          "exact_quote": "A uniform deterministic algorithm returns exactly the set $$U=\\bigl\\{v\\in V:\\exists\\text{ Max strategy }\\sigma\\ \n       \\forall\\text{ Min strategies }\\tau,\n       \\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{MP}}_w(\\pi_{v,\\sigma,\\tau})\\ge0\\bigr\\}$$ for every game specified above. For an absolute constant $C>0$, the complete computation uses at most $$2^{C(\\log_2(L+2))^2}$$ bit operations in a standard model polynomially equivalent to a uniform deterministic Turing machine.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-accepted-box",
        "exact_quote": "For the operator in Equation <a href=\"#eq:operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:operator\">[eq:operator]</a> and arbitrary finite integer bounds $A\\le u$, every subsolution $x$ and supersolution $y$ in $[A,u]$ satisfy $x_i\\le y_i$ for all $i\\in V$. There is a unique $h\\in[A,u]$ that is both a subsolution and a supersolution. It is precisely the unique fixed point of $$T_{A,u}(z)=\\min\\{u,\\max\\{A,F(z)\\}\\}.$$ Consequently every such pair satisfies $x\\le h\\le y$.*\n\n*The following synchronous iteration finds $h$. Start with $p=A$; evaluate $q=T_{A,u}(p)$; return $p$ if $q=p$, and otherwise set $p=q$ and repeat. It uses at most $$1+\\sum_{i\\in V}(u_i-A_i)$$ evaluations of $T_{A,u}$, including the final stability test.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-game-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h$ be the box solution in $[0,D_0]^V$. Then $$V_{\\mathrm{lo}}=\\{i:h_i\\le nW\\},\\qquad\n V_{\\mathrm{hi}}=\\{i:h_i\\ge D_0-nW\\}$$ partition $V$. There is a positional Max strategy under which every play starting in $V_{\\mathrm{hi}}$ has original mean payoff at least zero. There is a positional Min strategy under which every play starting in $V_{\\mathrm{lo}}$ has original upper limiting average at most $-1/n$. Both assertions allow arbitrary history-dependent opposition.",
        "source_id": "PM-104.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-translation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(A,D,a,b)$ be an input with $D\\ge4096$, let $c-M\\le k\\le c+M$ be integral, and let $r\\in\\{q,D/2\\}$. For any relevant Max witness $x$, the vector $$x_i'=\\max\\{k_i-r/2,\\ x_i-s_x(k)+r/2\\}\n \\label{eq:max-translation}$$ is an integer subsolution in $[k-r/2,k+r/2]$. Its support is $$S_{x'}=\\{i:g_i^x(k)<r\\}\\subseteq S_x.$$ For any positive new Max masses $a'$ with $a'(S_{x'})\\le1$, it is a Max witness and claims every $i$ with $g_i^x(k)\\le r/32$.*\n\n*For any relevant Min witness $y$, the vector $$y_i'=\\min\\{k_i+r/2,\\ y_i+s_y(k)-r/2\\}\n \\label{eq:min-translation}$$ is an integer supersolution in the same box, with support $$S_{y'}=\\{i:g_i^y(k)<r\\}\\subseteq S_y.$$ For any positive new Min masses $b'$ with $b'(S_{y'})\\le1$, it is a Min witness and claims every $i$ with $g_i^y(k)\\le r/32$. In particular, the corresponding unchanged masses $a$ and $b$ always make these translated vectors witnesses.",
        "source_id": "PM-104.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-full-solution",
        "exact_quote": "A uniform deterministic algorithm returns the exact value $\\operatorname{val}_w(v)$ as a reduced rational for every $v\\in V$ and one positional strategy $\\sigma^\\star$ for Max and one positional strategy $\\tau^\\star$ for Min such that, simultaneously for every $v\\in V$ and every pair of history-dependent strategies $\\sigma,\\tau$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{MP}}_w(\\pi_{v,\\sigma^\\star,\\tau})\\ge\\operatorname{val}_w(v),\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{MP}}_w(\\pi_{v,\\sigma,\\tau^\\star})\\le\\operatorname{val}_w(v).$$ The complete computation takes $2^{O((\\log_2(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations.",
        "source_id": "PM-104.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-full-solution",

          "source_id": "PM-104.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "A uniform deterministic algorithm returns the exact value $\\operatorname{val}_w(v)$ as a reduced rational for every $v\\in V$ and one positional strategy $\\sigma^\\star$ for Max and one positional strategy $\\tau^\\star$ for Min such that, simultaneously for every $v\\in V$ and every pair of history-dependent strategies $\\sigma,\\tau$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{MP}}_w(\\pi_{v,\\sigma^\\star,\\tau})\\ge\\operatorname{val}_w(v),\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{MP}}_w(\\pi_{v,\\sigma,\\tau^\\star})\\le\\operatorname{val}_w(v).$$ The complete computation takes $2^{O((\\log_2(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-energy-credits",
        "exact_quote": "Uniform deterministic algorithms have the following guarantees.*\n\n1.  *Given $v$ and a nonnegative binary integer $c$, decide whether $(v,c)$ is energy-winning. On a positive answer, return a positional Max policy satisfying Equation <a href=\"#eq:energy-objective\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:energy-objective\">[eq:energy-objective]</a> from that start and credit against every history-dependent Min strategy.*\n\n2.  *Given $v$, return the exact $c_{\\min}(v)$, including the possible answer $\\infty$. On a finite answer, return a positional Max policy winning from $v$ at credit $c_{\\min}(v)$.*\n\n*The fixed-credit computation takes $2^{O((\\log_2(L_c+2))^2)}$ bit operations, where $L_c$ is the full explicit input length including the start and credit. The minimum-credit computation takes $2^{O((\\log_2(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations for the original game length $L$. Handling every vertex by separate per-start searches, including a separate policy for every finite answer, has the same form of total bit bound.*\n\n*Each policy may depend on the queried start and credit; it also wins from that same start at every larger credit.",
        "source_id": "PM-104.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

On a finite answer, return a positional Max policy winning from $v$ at credit $c_{\\min}(v)$.*\n\n*The fixed-credit computation takes $2^{O((\\log_2(L_c+2))^2)}$ bit operations, where $L_c$ is the full explicit input length including the start and credit.         "exact_quote": "For explicitly listed $m\\times n$ matrices $A,B$ and $m$-dimensional vectors $c,d$ over $\\mathbb Q\\cup\\{-\\infty\\}$, feasibility of $$\\max\\bigl\\{c_i,\\max_j(A_{ij}+x_j)\\bigr\\}\n \\leq \\max\\bigl\\{d_i,\\max_j(B_{ij}+x_j)\\bigr\\}\n \\qquad\\text{for every row }i$$ is decidable deterministically in $2^{O((\\log(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations, where $L$ is the complete explicit binary input length. Variables range over $\\mathbb R\\cup\\{-\\infty\\}$; feasibility with all coordinates finite is decidable within the same bound. For a homogeneous system ($c=d=-\\infty$), one can also compute the maximal finite-coordinate support of a solution, including the empty support. In particular, the same bound decides feasibility of max-atom systems $z\\leq\\max(x,y)+a$, with rational constants $a$ and finite real, equivalently rational, variables.*\n\n*For each fixed integer $k\\geq0$, a deterministic algorithm with bit bound $2^{O_k((\\log(L+2))^2)}$ decides whether an explicitly listed $m\\times n$ matrix over $\\mathbb Q\\cup\\{-\\infty\\}$, with $m\\geq n\\geq1$, has tropical rank at least $n-k$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Mean payoff** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For an infinite play $\pi=(e_0,e_1,\ldots)$, write $$\mathop{\mathrm{MP}}_w(\pi)=\liminf_{T\to\infty}\frac1T \sum_{t=0}^{T-1}w(e_t).$$
- **Perturbed weights** ([source](#eq:cycle-signs)): Fix the input graph and ownership throughout. Following the integer cycle perturbation of Björklund and Vorobyov (Björklund and Vorobyov 2007, sec. 9) [[BjorklundVorobyov2007](#ref-BjorklundVorobyov2007)], put $$\widehat w(e)=(n+1)w(e)+1, \qquad W=\max_{e\in E}\|\widehat w(e)\|. \label{eq:bias}$$
- **Min-max potential operator** ([source](#eq:operator)): For all $z\in\mathbb Z^V$, define $$F_i(z)= \begin{cases} \displaystyle\max_{e:i\to j}(\widehat w(e)+z_j),&i\in V_{\mathrm{Max}},\\[2mm] \displaystyle\min_{e:i\to j}(\widehat w(e)+z_j),&i\in V_{\mathrm{Min}}. \end{cases} \label{eq:operator}$$
- **Integer box** ([source](#eq:operator)): For integer vectors $A\le u$, the notation $[A,u]$ denotes all integer vectors between them.
- **Subsolution** ([source](#eq:operator)): A *subsolution* is a full vector $x\in[A,u]$ satisfying $x_i\le F_i(x)$ at every coordinate where $x_i>A_i$.
- **Supersolution** ([source](#eq:operator)): A *supersolution* is a full vector $y\in[A,u]$ satisfying $y_i\ge F_i(y)$ wherever $y_i<u_i$.
- **Max witness** ([source](#stmt-def-witnesses)): A *Max witness* for $(A,D,a,b)$ is a subsolution $x\in[A,u]$ with support $S_x=\{i:x_i>A_i\}$ and $a(S_x)\le1$.
- **Min witness** ([source](#stmt-def-witnesses)): A *Min witness* is a supersolution $y\in[A,u]$ with support $S_y=\{i:y_i<u_i\}$ and $b(S_y)\le1$.
- **Relevant witness** ([source](#stmt-def-witnesses)): A witness is *relevant* when it claims at least one index.
- **Recursive input** ([source](#eq:recursive-input)): The recursion works on inputs $$A\in\mathbb Z^V,\qquad D=2^d\ (d\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}),\qquad a,b\in\mathbb Q_{>0}^V. \label{eq:recursive-input}$$
- **Max peak and gaps** ([source](#eq:max-gaps)): For a relevant Max witness $x$, let $$s_x(k)=\max_{i\in V}(x_i-k_i),\qquad g_i^x(k)=s_x(k)-(x_i-k_i). \label{eq:max-gaps}$$
- **Min peak and gaps** ([source](#eq:min-gaps)): For a relevant Min witness $y$ the dual definitions are $$s_y(k)=\max_{i\in V}(k_i-y_i),\qquad g_i^y(k)=s_y(k)-(k_i-y_i). \label{eq:min-gaps}$$
- **Game value** ([source](#sec:full-solution)): For the input model of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, write $$\operatorname{val}_w(v) =\sup_\sigma\inf_\tau \mathop{\mathrm{MP}}_w(\pi_{v,\sigma,\tau}),$$ where the strategies may depend on the full finite history.
- **Energy-winning start and credit** ([source](#eq:energy-objective)): For a start $v\in V$ and an integer credit $c\ge0$, call $(v,c)$ *energy-winning* if Max has a strategy such that, against every history-dependent Min strategy, the induced play satisfies $$c+\sum_{t=0}^{j-1}w(e_t)\ge0\qquad\text{for every }j\ge0. \label{eq:energy-objective}$$
- **Minimum energy credit** ([source](#eq:energy-objective)): Write $c_{\min}(v)$ for the least nonnegative integer credit for which $(v,c)$ is energy-winning, and put $c_{\min}(v)=\infty$ if there is none.
- **Tropical rank** ([source](#stmt-cor-tropical-computation)): Here tropical rank is the largest order of a square submatrix whose permutation sums have a finite, unique maximum. The rank is zero if no such nonempty submatrix exists.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q01 | Complete deterministic winning-set bit operations | ≤ | $2^{C(\log_2(L+2))^2}$ | An absolute constant C; the complete explicit binary input length L. |
| Q02 | Clipped-operator evaluations including the final stability test | ≤ | $1+\sum_{i\in V}(u_i-A_i)$ | As stated in the source. |
| Q03 | Initial dyadic width lower bound | ≥ | $64(n+1)(W+1)$ | The least qualifying power of two. |
| Q04 | Upper limiting average secured by Min in the low band | ≤ | $-1/n$ | All history-dependent opposing strategies. |
| Q05 | Claim margin | = | $D/32$ | As stated in the source. |
| Q06 | Pivot displacement bound | = | $D/8$ | Dyadic nonbase width. |
| Q07 | Narrow child width | = | $D/128$ | Dyadic nonbase width. |
| Q08 | Pivot step | = | $D/4096$ | Dyadic nonbase width. |
| Q09 | Child calls per pivot pass | ≤ | $1024n+1$ | Conditional on child termination. |
| Q10 | Minimum mass-product growth factor | = | $\frac{32}{25}$ | As stated in the source. |
| Q11 | Mass of unsettled Min near-peak support | > | $3/4$ | A Min witness with a claim outside the downward marks. |
| Q12 | Final near-peak support mass for unsettled witnesses | > | $3/4$ | The respective claim lies outside the corresponding override set. |
| Q13 | Favorable support mass multiplier | = | $\frac45$ | As stated in the source. |
| Q14 | Opposite support mass multiplier | = | $\frac85$ | As stated in the source. |
| Q15 | Complete main recursion call count | ≤ | $(H+1)(B_0+1)(1+Hs)^{B_0}$ | As stated in the source. |
| Q16 | Exact values and optimal strategies bit operations | ≤ | $2^{O((\log_2(L+2))^2)}$ | As stated in the source. |
| Q17 | Threshold calls for values and separate strategy reductions | ≤ | $nK(1+m)$ | The bisection count and original graph size from the full-solution reduction. |
| Q18 | Probability of the whole randomized output being correct | ≥ | $7/8$ | The entire exact value and strategy output. |
| Q19 | Known winning credit bound for starts with finite minimum credit | = | $(n-1)W_{\mathrm{in}}$ | May be zero; applies only after membership in the winning region is established. |
| Q20 | Fixed-corank tropical-rank bit operations | ≤ | $2^{O_k((\log(L+2))^2)}$ | Corank is fixed; the matrix is explicit with at least as many rows as columns. |

There is a unique $h\\in[A,u]$ that is both a subsolution and a supersolution. Variables range over $\\mathbb R\\cup\\{-\\infty\\}$; feasibility with all coordinates finite is decidable within the same bound. Equalities between the two displayed maxima are allowed, interpreted as pairs of inequalities. For a homogeneous system ($c=d=-\\infty$), one can also compute the maximal finite-coordinate support of a solution, including the empty support. In particular, the same bound decides feasibility of max-atom systems $z\\leq\\max(x,y)+a$, with rational constants $a$ and finite real, equivalently rational, variables.*\n\n*For each fixed integer $k\\geq0$, a deterministic algorithm with bit bound $2^{O_k((\\log(L+2))^2)}$ decides whether an explicitly listed $m\\times n$ matrix over $\\mathbb Q\\cup\\{-\\infty\\}$, with $m\\geq n\\geq1$, has tropical rank at least $n-k$. Here tropical rank is the largest order of a square submatrix whose permutation sums have a finite, unique maximum. The rank is zero if no such nonempty submatrix exists.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-accepted-box) / [proof](#sec:boxes): Use the finite-box comparison and synchronous iteration interface. -> Unique box solution and comparison inequalities
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-game-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-game-reduction): Follow tight potential edges to a boundary and delete simple cycles from consistent prefixes. -> Winning partition and positional strategies
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-translation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-translation): Translate by a common scalar and clip while using monotonicity and shift invariance. -> Contained witness supports and child claims
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-step-continuation) / [proof](#sec:algorithm): Execute exact base cases and ordered pivot passes, then reweight and continue. -> A complete label vector
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-termination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-termination): Induct on the decreasing width and mass-product rank. -> Termination independently of label correctness
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-down) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-down): Protect translated Max claims and use the final stationary child call. -> Safe downward marks and a Min mass certificate
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-up) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-up): Protect translated Min claims and preserve near-peak support while the pivot rises. -> Safe upward marks and final mass certificates
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-reweight) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reweight): Use provisional favorable labels on the heavy supports to bound reweighted witness mass. -> Survival of unsettled witnesses
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-labelling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reweight-2): Combine base cases, witness survival, continuation labels, and safe overrides by rank induction. -> Simultaneous obligations and the winning-set labelling
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-node-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-node-count): Count paths by depth and budget-decreasing positions, then account for polynomial local bit cost. -> Quasipolynomial complete deterministic bit bound
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-cor-full-solution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-full-solution): Bisect rational thresholds, reconstruct bounded-denominator values, and restrict each player separately while preserving all values. -> Exact values and globally optimal strategies
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-cor-randomized-full-solution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-randomized-full-solution): Amplify each adaptive threshold query with fresh independent runs and bound the first error. -> Whole-output randomized success guarantee
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-cor-energy-credits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-energy-credits): Use adversary-controlled resets and binary search over the finite credit bound. -> Fixed-credit decision, exact minimum credit and per-start policy
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-cor-tropical-computation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-tropical-computation): Scale rational data, preprocess forced infinite coordinates, solve a bipartite game, and test selected column sets. -> Feasibility, maximal finite support and fixed-corank rank decision

          "exact_quote": "For the operator in Equation <a href=\"#eq:operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:operator\">[eq:operator]</a> and arbitrary finite integer bounds $A\\le u$, every subsolution $x$ and supersolution $y$ in $[A,u]$ satisfy $x_i\\le y_i$ for all $i\\in V$. There is a unique $h\\in[A,u]$ that is both a subsolution and a supersolution. It is precisely the unique fixed point of $$T_{A,u}(z)=\\min\\{u,\\max\\{A,F(z)\\}\\}.$$ Consequently every such pair satisfies $x\\le h\\le y$.*\n\n*The following synchronous iteration finds $h$. Start with $p=A$; evaluate $q=T_{A,u}(p)$; return $p$ if $q=p$, and otherwise set $p=q$ and repeat. It uses at most $$1+\\sum_{i\\in V}(u_i-A_i)$$ evaluations of $T_{A,u}$, including the final stability test.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-box**: The box solution is the unique vector that is both a subsolution and a supersolution; synchronous iteration from the lower endpoint finds it.
- **I-recursion**: Every recursive child has smaller rank, while simultaneous witness obligations determine the required labels.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-width**: Use wide-box synchronous iteration as the complete binary-length solver. -> The iteration count can depend on the numerical width.
- **A-edges**: Choose any edge connecting vertices with equal value as an optimal strategy. -> An equal-value edge can still produce a smaller payoff.
- **A-rank**: Claim the fixed-corank complexity bound for unrestricted corank. -> The number of column-subset tests depends on the corank.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite-box synchronous iteration is an explicit finite constructive subroutine; this check does not implement the complete quasipolynomial recursion.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of total finite games with owners, edges [tail,head,weight], A and u.
# PRECONDITIONS: signed integer weights; owner Max or Min; integer A<=u; explicit nonempty outgoing lists.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of unique integer box solutions in input order.
# TIME: O((1+sum(u-A))*(n+m)) exact arithmetic operations per game.
# SPACE: O(n+m) exact integers per game, plus input/output storage.
# ARITHMETIC: arbitrary precision certified integer operations using fmpz.
# FAILURE: malformed input or an exceeded iteration cap raises and exits nonzero.
import json,sys
from flint import fmpz
def basic(g):
    n=len(g['owners'])
    assert n>0 and all(v in ('Max','Min') for v in g['owners'])
    A=list(map(fmpz,g['A'])); u=list(map(fmpz,g['u']))
    assert len(A)==len(u)==n and all(A[i]<=u[i] for i in range(n))
    outgoing=[[] for _ in range(n)]
    for i,j,w in g['edges']:
        assert type(i)==type(j)==type(w)==int and 0<=i<n and 0<=j<n
        outgoing[i].append((j,(n+1)*fmpz(w)+1))
    assert all(outgoing)
    z=A[:]
    cap=1+sum(int(u[i]-A[i]) for i in range(n))
    for _ in range(cap):
        q=[]
        for i in range(n):
            terms=[w+z[j] for j,w in outgoing[i]]
            extremum=max(terms) if g['owners'][i]=='Max' else min(terms)
            q.append(min(u[i],max(A[i],extremum)))
        if q==z:
            return [int(t) for t in z]
        z=q
    raise RuntimeError('iteration cap exceeded')
print(json.dumps([basic(g) for g in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

    "input_contract": "A JSON list of total explicit games, each with owners Max/Min, integer edges [tail,head,weight] using zero-based vertices, and integer vectors A<=u. The implemented algorithm is Basic; exhaustive oracle coverage is restricted to the listed small inputs.",
    "language": "python",
    "mutants": [
      {
        "expected_failure": "A mixed-choice game returns the wrong box solution.",
        "id": "M1",
        "mutation": "Reverse owner extrema",
        "observed_exit": 1,
        "receipt_sha256": "6084cd65da1e9baeeea533fa8dd4113154d04b2231f9e671bf9bab77ed811c25"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "A zero-weight cycle receives the wrong sign and box solution.",
        "id": "M2",
        "mutation": "Reverse the perturbation bias",
        "observed_exit": 1,
        "receipt_sha256": "5c9b564fdfbdc5bfc22c7f8fb9e7a9ab03370c4c31dee9eb1ff7706ade00140b"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "Boundary and collapsed-box solutions disagree with exact enumeration.",
        "id": "M3",
        "mutation": "Lower the clipping upper endpoint",
        "observed_exit": 1,
        "receipt_sha256": "3999efac9ad8e671a49215f8c45549850dc372c675f58d28a873fc124d11f978"
      }
    ],
    "oracle_code": "import itertools,json,sys\ninstances=json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\nreported=json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\nassert len(reported)==len(instances)\ntruth=[]\nfor game, answer in zip(instances,reported):\n    size=len(game['owners'])\n    solutions=[]\n    for point in itertools.product(*(range(lo,hi+1) for lo,hi in zip(game['A'],game['u']))):\n        valid=True\n        for vertex,player in enumerate(game['owners']):\n            alternatives=[(size+1)*weight+1+point[head] for tail,head,weight in game['edges'] if tail==vertex]\n            value=sorted(alternatives)[-1 if player=='Max' else 0]\n            if point[vertex]>game['A'][vertex] and point[vertex]>value:\n                valid=False\n            if point[vertex]<game['u'][vertex] and point[vertex]<value:\n                valid=False\n        if valid:\n            solutions.append(list(point))\n    assert len(solutions)==1, 'unique simultaneous sub/supersolution required'\n    assert all(type(item)==int for item in answer), 'exact integers required'\n    assert answer==solutions[0], 'box solution mismatch'\n    truth.append(solutions[0])\nassert truth==json.loads(sys.argv[3]), 'incorrect declared expected result'\n",
    "output_contract": "A JSON list of exact integer box solutions in input order. No winning-set or quasipolynomial runtime certificate is returned.",
    "reason": "The finite-box synchronous iteration is an explicit finite constructive subroutine; this check does not implement the complete quasipolynomial recursion.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of total finite games with owners, edges [tail,head,weight], A and u.\n# PRECONDITIONS: signed integer weights; owner Max or Min; integer A<=u; explicit nonempty outgoing lists.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list of unique integer box solutions in input order.\n# TIME: O((1+sum(u-A))*(n+m)) exact arithmetic operations per game.\n# SPACE: O(n+m) exact integers per game, plus input/output storage.\n# ARITHMETIC: arbitrary precision certified integer operations using fmpz.\n# FAILURE: malformed input or an exceeded iteration cap raises and exits nonzero.\nimport json,sys\nfrom flint import fmpz\ndef basic(g):\n    n=len(g['owners'])\n    assert n>0 and all(v in ('Max','Min') for v in g['owners'])\n    A=list(map(fmpz,g['A'])); u=list(map(fmpz,g['u']))\n    assert len(A)==len(u)==n and all(A[i]<=u[i] for i in range(n))\n    outgoing=[[] for _ in range(n)]\n    for i,j,w in g['edges']:\n        assert type(i)==type(j)==type(w)==int and 0<=i<n and 0<=j<n\n        outgoing[i].append((j,(n+1)*fmpz(w)+1))\n    assert all(outgoing)\n    z=A[:]\n    cap=1+sum(int(u[i]-A[i]) for i in range(n))\n    for _ in range(cap):\n        q=[]\n        for i in range(n):\n            terms=[w+z[j] for j,w in outgoing[i]]\n            extremum=max(terms) if g['owners'][i]=='Max' else min(terms)\n            q.append(min(u[i],max(A[i],extremum)))\n        if q==z:\n            return [int(t) for t in z]\n        z=q\n    raise RuntimeError('iteration cap exceeded')\nprint(json.dumps([basic(g) for g in json.load(sys.stdin)]))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | implementable | inferred | A game-solving component can return the entire winning set and, through the stated reductions, exact values and globally optimal positional strategies when its input matches the finite explicit game model. |
| games_simulation | True | strong | implementable | inferred | Applies to adversarial mean-payoff games on finite directed graphs and to the stated one-resource energy objective; it computes guarantees against arbitrary history-dependent opposition. |
| tools | True | strong | implementable | inferred | An exact decision tool can expose winning-set, value, strategy, and energy-credit queries under the stated input contracts. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | stated | The paper specifies a uniform deterministic recursion with exact arithmetic, simultaneous witness obligations, bounded pivot passes, and separate width and mass progress. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The explicit edge list and per-call records provide an implementation layout for this solver; the paper does not introduce a general-purpose data-structure theorem. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper gives a uniform bit algorithm and sequential exact implementation, without an accelerator design or hardware throughput guarantee. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the game-solving guarantees do not supply cryptographic primitives, adversarial protocol security, or security assumptions. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the stated inputs are explicit weighted games, not learned transition models or statistical estimators. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The minimum-credit computation provides an exact resource requirement for a specified start in a finite adversarial transition graph; applicability requires precisely the stated one-resource energy semantics. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the implementation runs recursive children sequentially and establishes no distributed execution or communication guarantee. |
| other (Tropical algebra) | True | strong | implementable | stated | Explicit rational tropical inequalities admit deterministic feasibility and maximal-support decisions; tropical rank testing is covered when the corank parameter is fixed. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The game must be finite, total, and partitioned between Max and Min. Blocked use: ['Applying the game theorem directly to dead ends or vertices without a unique owner.']
- **L02** (hypothesis): The direct game algorithm accepts signed binary integer weights and an explicit edge list. Blocked use: ['Claiming the direct game bound for arbitrary real weights or succinctly represented graphs.']
- **L03** (approximation): The complexity guarantee counts bit operations against the complete explicit binary input length; arithmetic-operation counts alone do not establish it. Blocked use: ['Replacing the bit bound with an unqualified unit-cost arithmetic bound.']
- **L04** (unknown): A polynomial bound in the complete binary input length is not established by this paper. Blocked use: ['Advertising the presented algorithm as polynomial or strongly polynomial.']
- **L05** (hypothesis): The finite-box comparison uses the specified integer operator after a perturbation excluding zero simple-cycle sums. Blocked use: ['Transferring uniqueness to arbitrary monotone operators or unperturbed games with zero-sum cycles.']
- **L06** (edge_case): Finite-box comparison includes negative endpoints and collapsed coordinates. Blocked use: ['Discarding collapsed coordinates or treating negative endpoints as outside the finite-box interface.']
- **L07** (edge_case): Witness translation guarantees support containment, without requiring strict containment. Blocked use: ['Using strict support shrinkage as a recursion progress invariant.']
- **L08** (edge_case): Labels at vertices without witness claims may be chosen freely within the simultaneous labelling guarantee. Blocked use: ['Interpreting an arbitrary internal call label as an exact game winner without the initial-box reduction.']
- **L09** (edge_case): Basic iteration is a finite-box procedure whose evaluation bound depends on numerical box width; the main recursion uses it only at bounded width. Blocked use: ['Using wide-box Basic iteration as an implementation of the quasipolynomial bit bound.']
- **L10** (edge_case): An edge between vertices of equal value need not be optimal; strategy recovery tests restrictions preserving the entire value vector. Blocked use: ['Choosing optimal edges solely by equality of endpoint values.', 'Recovering Min only from the Max-restricted game.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The energy-credit reduction concerns a single accumulated resource without an upper bound or saturation. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated credit guarantee directly to multiple resources, capacities, or saturating updates.']

Blocked use: ['Extending the coefficient domain to arbitrary real input encodings.', 'Claiming the fixed-corank bit bound uniformly for unrestricted corank.']

- **L15** (approximation): The randomized full solution has a success guarantee for the entire output, and its running-time bound holds on every random tape. On a finite answer, return a positional Max policy winning from $v$ at credit $c_{\\min}(v)$.*\n\n*The fixed-credit computation takes $2^{O((\\log_2(L_c+2))^2)}$ bit operations, where $L_c$ is the full explicit input length including the start and credit. The minimum-credit computation takes $2^{O((\\log_2(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations for the original game length $L$. Handling every vertex by separate per-start searches, including a separate policy for every finite answer, has the same form of total bit bound.*\n\n*Each policy may depend on the queried start and credit; it also wins from that same start at every larger credit.",
          "quote_sha256": "2648cc4ff646445d16f7f2fa69fd6f3648e53b9e989594b2f8c8a087d40ac537"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "There is one accumulated resource without an upper bound or saturation.",
      "question": "What initial credit is necessary to win a one-resource energy game?",

    },

      "answer_anchor": "stmt-cor-tropical-computation",

      "evidence": [

          "quote": "For explicitly listed $m\\times n$ matrices $A,B$ and $m$-dimensional vectors $c,d$ over $\\mathbb Q\\cup\\{-\\infty\\}$, feasibility of $$\\max\\bigl\\{c_i,\\max_j(A_{ij}+x_j)\\bigr\\}\n \\leq \\max\\bigl\\{d_i,\\max_j(B_{ij}+x_j)\\bigr\\}\n \\qquad\\text{for every row }i$$ is decidable deterministically in $2^{O((\\log(L+2))^2)}$ bit operations, where $L$ is the complete explicit binary input length. Variables range over $\\mathbb R\\cup\\{-\\infty\\}$; feasibility with all coordinates finite is decidable within the same bound. Equalities between the two displayed maxima are allowed, interpreted as pairs of inequalities. For a homogeneous system ($c=d=-\\infty$), one can also compute the maximal finite-coordinate support of a solution, including the empty support. In particular, the same bound decides feasibility of max-atom systems $z\\leq\\max(x,y)+a$, with rational constants $a$ and finite real, equivalently rational, variables.*\n\n*For each fixed integer $k\\geq0$, a deterministic algorithm with bit bound $2^{O_k((\\log(L+2))^2)}$ decides whether an explicitly listed $m\\times n$ matrix over $\\mathbb Q\\cup\\{-\\infty\\}$, with $m\\geq n\\geq1$, has tropical rank at least $n-k$. Here tropical rank is the largest order of a square submatrix whose permutation sums have a finite, unique maximum. The rank is zero if no such nonempty submatrix exists.",

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 104 (`lean/docs/104.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Deterministic quasipolynomial-time mean-payoff games](../../preprints/Deterministic-quasipolynomial-time-mean-payoff-games-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Randomized quasipolynomial-time mean-payoff games](../../preprints/Randomized-quasipolynomial-time-mean-payoff-games-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization checks that a specified two-step execution of Truffet's elimination procedure terminates with a feasible but nonoptimal output. It is a finite counterexample to that proposed optimization step.
>
> The formalized result gives one randomized algorithm for the complete zero-threshold winning set of every finite mean-payoff game with signed binary weights. It succeeds with probability at least $7/8$ and runs in quasipolynomial bit time on every random tape. Self-loops, parallel edges, and history-dependent strategies are allowed, and winning means a nonnegative liminf average payoff.

Comparator source `TruffetCounterexample.lean` (4547 bytes, sha256 `03fe8de8feab6eeb5144931898f079a0f900e695bf3f8c5d3de3dbc177e21169`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
namespace TruffetV4Counterexample

abbrev Coeff := WithBot ℝ

abbrev Form := Fin 3 → Coeff

abbrev Var := Fin 2

abbrev RowIndex := Fin 2

def column (j : Var) : Fin 3 := ⟨j.val, Nat.lt_trans j.isLt (by decide)⟩

def nullForm : Form := fun _ => ⊥

structure Row where
  lhs : Form
  rhs : Form

structure State where
  rows : RowIndex → Row
  cost : Form
  remaining : Var → Prop

def wellFormed (s : State) : Prop := ∀ j, ¬ s.remaining j →
  s.cost (column j) = ⊥ ∧ ∀ i,
    (s.rows i).lhs (column j) = ⊥ ∧ (s.rows i).rhs (column j) = ⊥

def eval (f : Form) (w : Fin 3 → ℝ) : Coeff :=
  max (f 0 + (w 0 : Coeff)) (max (f 1 + (w 1 : Coeff)) (f 2 + (w 2 : Coeff)))

def feasible (s : State) (w : Fin 3 → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ i, eval (s.rows i).rhs w ≤ eval (s.rows i).lhs w

def lower (s : State) (i : RowIndex) (j : Var) : Prop :=
  (s.rows i).lhs (column j) ≠ ⊥ ∧
    (s.rows i).rhs (column j) < (s.rows i).lhs (column j)

def upper (s : State) (i : RowIndex) (j : Var) : Prop :=
  (s.rows i).rhs (column j) ≠ ⊥ ∧
    (s.rows i).lhs (column j) < (s.rows i).rhs (column j)

def dominating (s : State) (j : Var) : Prop :=
  s.remaining j ∧ (∃ i, lower s i j) ∧ ∃ i, upper s i j

def eligible (s : State) (i : RowIndex) (j : Var) : Prop :=
  s.remaining j ∧ lower s i j ∧ ((∃ k, dominating s k) → dominating s j)

def bounded (s : State) : Prop :=
  ∀ j, s.remaining j → (∃ i, lower s i j) ∨ ∃ i, upper s i j

def stop (s : State) : Prop := ∀ i, (s.rows i).rhs 2 ≤ (s.rows i).lhs 2

def switch (s : State) : Prop :=
  (∀ j, s.remaining j → s.cost (column j) = ⊥) ∧ ¬ stop s ∧
  (∃ j, s.remaining j) ∧
  (¬ ∃ i j, s.remaining j ∧ lower s i j) ∧
  ∃ i j, s.remaining j ∧ upper s i j

def failure (s : State) : Prop := ¬ stop s ∧
  ((¬ ∃ j, s.remaining j) ∨
   ((¬ ∃ i j, s.remaining j ∧ lower s i j) ∧
    (¬ ∃ i j, s.remaining j ∧ upper s i j)))

def lowerForm (s : State) (i : RowIndex) (j : Var) : Form := fun k =>
  if k = column j then ⊥ else
    (s.rows i).rhs k + ((-WithBot.unbotD 0 ((s.rows i).lhs (column j)) : ℝ) : Coeff)

def substitute (f : Form) (j : Var) (g : Form) : Form := fun k =>
  if k = column j then ⊥ else max (f k) (f (column j) + g k)

def cleanup (r : Row) : Row := by
  classical
  exact if ∀ k, r.rhs k ≤ r.lhs k then ⟨nullForm, nullForm⟩ else r

def advance (s : State) (i : RowIndex) (j : Var) : State where
  rows := fun r => cleanup ⟨substitute (s.rows r).lhs j (lowerForm s i j),
    substitute (s.rows r).rhs j (lowerForm s i j)⟩
  cost := substitute s.cost j (lowerForm s i j)
  remaining := fun k => s.remaining k ∧ k ≠ j

def ForcedSingletonStep (s s' : State) (i : RowIndex) (j : Var) : Prop :=
  wellFormed s ∧ wellFormed s' ∧
  bounded s ∧ ¬ stop s ∧ ¬ switch s ∧ ¬ failure s ∧
  (∀ r k, eligible s r k ↔ r = i ∧ k = j) ∧
  (∃ k, (substitute s.cost j (lowerForm s i j)) k ≠ ⊥) ∧
  s' = advance s i j

def initial : State where
  rows := ![⟨![(0 : Coeff), 0, ⊥], ![⊥, ⊥, (0 : Coeff)]⟩,
    ⟨![(0 : Coeff), ⊥, ⊥], ![⊥, (1 : Coeff), ((-2 : ℝ) : Coeff)]⟩]
  cost := ![(0 : Coeff), ⊥, ⊥]
  remaining := fun _ => True

def middle : State where
  rows := ![⟨nullForm, nullForm⟩,
    ⟨![(0 : Coeff), ⊥, ⊥], ![⊥, ⊥, (1 : Coeff)]⟩]
  cost := ![(0 : Coeff), ⊥, ⊥]
  remaining := fun j => j = 0

def finalState : State where
  rows := fun _ => ⟨nullForm, nullForm⟩
  cost := ![⊥, ⊥, (1 : Coeff)]
  remaining := fun _ => False

def crossRowRestriction (s : State) : Prop :=
  ∀ i r : RowIndex, i ≠ r → ∀ α : ℝ, ∃ w : Fin 3 → ℝ,
    eval (s.rows i).lhs w < (α : Coeff) + eval (s.rows r).rhs w

def output : Fin 3 → ℝ := ![1, 0, 0]

def better : Fin 3 → ℝ := ![0, -1, 0]

theorem finite_execution_not_optimal :
    crossRowRestriction initial ∧
    ForcedSingletonStep initial middle 0 1 ∧
    ForcedSingletonStep middle finalState 1 0 ∧
    (¬ ∃ j, finalState.remaining j) ∧ stop finalState ∧
    feasible initial output ∧ feasible initial better ∧
    output 2 = 0 ∧ better 2 = 0 ∧
    (output 1 : Coeff) = eval (lowerForm initial 0 1) output ∧
    (output 0 : Coeff) = eval (lowerForm middle 1 0) output ∧
    eval initial.cost output = eval finalState.cost output ∧
    eval initial.cost better < eval finalState.cost output := by
  sorry

end TruffetV4Counterexample
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a deterministic algorithm that computes the complete zero-threshold winning set of a finite mean-payoff game with arbitrary signed integer edge weights encoded in binary. For total explicit input length <i>L</i>, it uses $`2^{O((\log(L+2))^2)}`$ bit operations. A reduction also computes the exact rational value at every vertex and globally optimal positional strategies for both players within the same quasipolynomial bound.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
